# ChangeLog

> Curated, user-facing summary of completed work, newest first.

## [Unreleased]

- **Every report, ranked by model** (`docs/reports.md`): the reports of `janas-bench` pasted into the issues, 45 so far, ranked for each model by the speed of a reply, made by `tools/reports_table` from the issues (`src/common/bench_report.c` reads a report, `tests/test_bench_report` cuts it short at every byte and mutates it 20,000 times). `janas-bench --share` prints the address of a new issue with the report filled in, and opens it in the browser if the user says yes; nothing is sent until the user sends the issue.
- **A card with memory of its own reads ahead** (issue #63): with every pass whole on the card, a token's experts that neither the card nor the cache held were read when their layer asked for them, the card waiting. Now, when the CPU gets a layer's state for its share of the experts, it ranks the next layer's with that layer's router (the norm of one layer undone, the next one's applied) and starts the reads of the likeliest not on the card, as the CPU's path does (`JANAS_PREFETCH` holds for both). Qwen3-Next-80B with a 4 GiB cache and the integrated GPU acting as a card of 2.5 GiB (`JANAS_GPU_COPY=1`), two alternated pairs on a quiet machine: experts missed a token 69 to 31, the card's wait for the cache 39 to 21 ms a pass, writing 6.1 to 6.8 tok/s. On that laptop the shared path stays faster (11.8 tok/s): its integrated GPU is slow at the card's own work.
- **The model's line says a forecast as one** (issue #50): "every pass whole on the card" was said when the model opened, and stayed when the card then refused the first pass; until a pass has run there it now says "once the first one runs there".
- **`build.bat` on Windows itself** (issue #64): MSYS2's binutils 2.47 has `windres` unprefixed only, and the resources' step failed; `build.sh` takes it where the prefixed one is missing. The first build on a real Windows machine (a Ryzen 5 8645HS) found it.

- **Releases and the develop branch** (`docs/releases.md`): `main` stays at the last release and `develop` takes each fix as it is done; how to get develop, with git or as a ZIP, and build it on Linux and Windows.
- **Downloads behind a proxy** (issue #70): `janas-get`, Janas's services and the MCP servers the chat reaches take the proxy from the environment as curl does (`https_proxy`/`HTTPS_PROXY`, `http_proxy`, `all_proxy`/`ALL_PROXY`, `no_proxy`/`NO_PROXY`; an `http://` proxy, with a user and password if given, port 1080 when none is), through a CONNECT for https. A VM that reached the Internet only through a proxy stopped at "huggingface.co: Name or service not known". Not for the MCP servers a request to `janas-server` names, which must be public addresses: through a proxy that cannot be checked, so those go directly. Tried with `tests/test_proxy` (a proxy and a server of its own) and with a model downloaded from Hugging Face through a local proxy, its CDN redirect included.

## [2026-11-13] - Conversations saved, taken up and exported; AMD's NPUs named

- **Conversations saved, taken up and exported** (issue #69): every conversation of `janas-chat` is saved as it goes, a file each in `~/.local/share/janas/chats` (`--no-save`: none), with the replies' reasoning and tool calls, the model, the settings and a title (the first question's, or `/rename`'s); `janas-chat --continue` takes up the last one, with its model when none is given, `--resume` and `/resume` choose one in a menu of arrows (title, date, model, messages), and the conversation's last messages are shown again; `/export` writes it in Markdown or plain text (`--title`, `--overwrite`), `/import` reads either back, or the saved form. In the library for every program: `janas_llm_chat_messages`, `_message`, `_title`, `_autosave`, `_save`, `_restore`, `_export`, `janas_llm_chats_list`, `janas_llm_chat_file_info` (a conversation taken up is loaded whole with its next message, its oldest exchanges left out of what the model reads when it does not fit), in `llm.bi`, in `janas_oop.bi` (`chat.Messages`, `SaveTo`, `RestoreFrom`, `ExportTo`...) and in the Node module (`chat.messages()`, `autosave`, `save`, `restore`, `export`, `janas.listChats()`); the web chat of `janas-server --test` exports and imports the same Markdown and keeps its last 50 conversations in the browser. Tried with Qwen3-0.6B: a conversation saved, taken up with `--continue` and asked about what came before, exported, imported, in janas-chat (also under AddressSanitizer), through Node, FreeBASIC and the web page.
- **A request on the side in a new conversation**: `janas_llm_chat_aside` failed with "out of memory" when nothing had been computed yet (right after a reset, or a conversation taken up); it now asks on an empty session and leaves it empty.
- **AMD's NPUs named** (issue #64): where the machine has an AMD NPU, janas-bench's `NPU:` line and the engine's reason name it, its generation (XDNA: Phoenix, Hawk Point; XDNA 2: Strix, Krackan Point) and its driver (Linux, from sysfs) or its device (Windows, SetupAPI), and say that Janas does not use AMD's NPUs yet, where it said there was none.
- **`janas-setup serve --monitor`**: each server it starts serves its page of the engine at work too (`/monitor`).
- **The extension for VS Code and VSCodium, 0.1.2**: a model downloaded with its progress in a notification that can cancel it, the setting up going on by itself (it ran in a terminal that had to be closed by hand); once set up, which model to choose in Continue; the first start's last step says how to have the code written into a file (Apply, Ctrl+I, Agent); "Janas: set up" with the editor's warning background; **Janas: Show the engine at work**, the servers started with `--monitor` where janas-setup knows it (setting `janas.monitor`), plain where it does not; the servers stopped said so in the log; Windows paths with their drive in capitals.

## [2026-11-12] - The engine at work, further: where the time goes and inside the layers; Gemma 4 after a long reply

- **Gemma 4 after a long reply, fixed**: a sliding-window layer keeps only its last positions, in a ring; when a conversation went back to an earlier point (a server's client sends the whole conversation each time, and Janas keeps what the new one shares with the last), the ring could already hold the positions of the reply that came after, and the model read them as the window. The replies came out wrong, empty or made of `<pad>`: on MATH (lm-evaluation-harness, 70 problems) Gemma 4 E2B answered 36 with nothing and solved 1.4%; with the ring checked, none empty and 44.3%. A session now goes back only as far as the ring still holds, and computes the conversation again otherwise (`janas_llm_model_rewind_ok`); `llm_session` checks a conversation rewound past a long reply against a fresh one.
- **The monitor looks further** (discussion 61): where the engine's time goes, the phases of its passes as a bar (attention, the feed-forward and the experts, the output head, the recurrent layers), and what the tuner chose for each kind of pass, from counters the engine keeps anyway; and, while a page asks for it (`/monitor/events?layers=1`, the page's **Look inside the layers**, `/monitor layers` in `janas-chat`), a look inside the layers: the feed-forward's neurons and the residual after each layer in 32 groups, a row a layer, and for a mixture the experts chosen and read from the disk, layer by layer (`src/llm/model_monitor.c`, an observer of the forward pass, with a new `route` hook where the expert cache says whether an expert was in memory). `janas_llm_monitor_layers_on`, `_shape` and `janas_llm_monitor_layers` in `llm.h` and `llm.bi`; `janas_llm_monitor` gains `phase_seconds`, `choices` and `layers_on`. What it costs while on: Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B wrote 2% slower (29.4 tok/s against 29.9, six alternated pairs), Qwen3-0.6B within the noise; reading a prompt, no cost above the noise.
- **How a model larger than the memory runs** (`docs/how-it-works.md`): the bytes a token needs, read from Qwen3-Next-80B's file (48 layers of 512 experts, 10 a token, 2.04 MB each), the page cache's way against the expert cache (21.4 GiB, 94.8% of the experts found, about 50 MB from the SSD a token), reading ahead, bit planes, drafts, the three ways for a prompt and the tuner, each with its measured figure; three figures for both themes.
- **The extension for VS Code and VSCodium, 0.1.1**: a first start opens a page of four steps (set up Janas, a model, an extension to chat and complete code with, a first question), each ticking itself when done; until Janas is set up every start says so, and the status bar reads "Janas: set up"; a start with no extension to chat with says that too. The setting up no longer stops in silence (on Windows 11 with VS Code 1.136, 0.1.0 stopped after the list of the models, saying nothing): every step is in the log, and a choice not made or an installation failed is said, with a way to go on. Janas's folder taken away is a first start again. An icon, janas-chat's octopus, and a changelog.
- **Folders made on Windows with its own paths**: `janas-get --dir C:\...\models` stopped with "cannot make" when the folder above was missing too, because the folders on the way were found only at `/`; now at `\` too, the drive skipped. Found on Windows 11 by trying `janas-get` on a fresh folder.
- **A second model in the same process gets every core**: opening a model pins the calling thread to one core for the model's life, and a model opened after it in the same thread saw that one core and made its threads for it alone: `janas-server`'s embedding model (`--embedding-model`), opened after the chat model, was made with one thread. A thread pinned by Janas now offers a later model the cores it had before; `test_pool` checks it.

## [2026-11-10] - Janas in VS Code and VSCodium in one command, the engine at work in a page

- **`janas-setup`**: `janas-setup editor` finds VS Code and VSCodium and their extensions, proposes a model for code and one for completion from the machine's memory and the models installed (the same one when memory holds one), sets up Continue (a file of its own beside the user's configuration), llama.vscode, Cline and VS Code's chat, or installs the extension for what the user wants from the editor's own gallery; `--undo` puts every file back byte for byte, `janas-setup serve` starts the servers. Run on Linux and under Wine with Qwen3-0.6B ([docs/editor.md](docs/editor.md)).
- **`--editor`**: `janas-setup editor --editor vscode|vscodium` sets up that editor only. `--code` and `--complete` take the catalog's names too (`qwen3-0.6b`), and the proposal is made around the model named.
- **`janas-server --monitor`** (discussion 61): `/monitor` shows the engine at work, one sample a second: the latest reply (speeds, first token, drafts, threads), its prompt by where the tokens come from, the expert cache (found in memory, read from the disk, read ahead and used, reads against waits; the card's tier where there is one), memory, context, the threads busy and on Linux the processor's watts, charts of the last five minutes and the last 16 replies. `/monitor` in `janas-chat` shows the same as a page of the terminal, made anew every second. Counters and times only, never the text. The library gives them from any thread: `janas_llm_monitor` and `janas_llm_monitor_reply` (`llm.h`, `llm.bi`), a copy of the counters each reply keeps, made under a lock at every step.
- **A menu of the models in `janas-chat`** (issue #62): started with no model in a terminal, the chat shows the models here and the ones to download in two lists, moved through with the arrows; I says what a model is and the memory it is meant for against the machine's, Enter opens it or offers to download it, Esc leaves without opening any. The models here include any other chat model in the folder, found as `janas-setup` finds them (`src/common/model_scan.c`, from `src/setup/models.c`). Piped, the numbered list stays.
- **Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct in the catalog**: `janas-get qwen3-coder-30b-a3b`, converted, from `prabanta-dev/Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct-JNS`.
- **The extension for VS Code and VSCodium** (`editors/vscode/`): installs Janas in its own folder (`~/.local/share/janas`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\janas`), the package checked against its published SHA-256 and its `SHA256SUMS`; lists the catalog for the machine's memory, the models for code first; installs Continue, llama.vscode or Cline where none is there; runs `janas-setup editor` and `serve`; once a day says when a new version is out, installs it only when asked and keeps the one before to go back to. Run in VSCodium with Qwen3-0.6B; the installation against the release v2026.11.07.
- **Settings changed in place**: `src/common/jsonc.c` changes one member of a JSON file with comments, as VS Code's settings are, every other byte kept; a broken file is left alone (`test_jsonc`, `test_setup`).

## [2026-11-07] - The CPU alone for a prompt where it is faster, one thread per core first

- **The CPU alone for a prompt, where it is faster** (issue #50): with a GPU, its fast prompt read a prompt's blocks in every configuration, and the CPU alone was never measured on them; a Ryzen 9 9900X with its 512-bit tiles read Qwen3.6-35B-A3B's prompt at 413 tok/s without the GPU and at 346 with an RTX 5060 Ti. The engine now tries the CPU alone on a prompt's blocks too and keeps it where it is faster; the side, the CPU alone or the fast prompt, is settled by the first tries with the threads and kept, since their sums differ in the last bits. janas-bench shows these rows as "CPU alone", a prompt's speed only. On the development laptop the integrated GPU's fast prompt stayed ahead on Qwen3-4B (134-149 tok/s against 109-133).
- **One thread per core first, where every core has two** (issue #24): on a processor whose threads all sit on cores with a sibling and no efficiency cores (the Ryzens, and Intel's processors without efficiency cores), a reply and a prompt's blocks now start on one thread per core, the tuner still trying both. Of nine testers' processors one thread per core wrote a reply faster in every run, by 5-54% (a Ryzen 7 9800X3D 27.6 tok/s against 20.1), and read a prompt up to 35% faster, where both threads of a core won by 17% at most; the 9800X3D's first runs had given a prompt's blocks every thread, 70.6 tok/s against 149.5.
- **Qwen3-Next whole on a card, against the CPU**: with the integrated Arc acting as a card of its own (`JANAS_GPU_COPY=1`) Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B's whole pass was run with `llm_eval` on two texts of 400 tokens against the CPU alone: the most likely token the same at 384 and 388 positions of 400, the logits apart by 0.10 on average, as far as the fast prompt's tiles are (246 of 256 positions in blocks of 100, 0.09-0.10 apart); a token at a time without the whole pass the GPU gives the CPU's bits, 400 of 400. Which of the two is nearer llama.cpp's is not measured (its oracle for the 80B was not at hand).
- **The cache giving way to the card's tier, measured**: on the same runs, with a tier of 2 GiB and a cache of 9000 MiB, `JANAS_GPU_DEMOTE` 1 and 0 alternated gave 76.9% and 76.7% of the experts from memory and 5.7-5.8 tokens/s both: no difference to be seen on this laptop, where the card's memory is the same memory.
- **The README warns of a site using Janas' name**: `joaniberian9588.github.io` offers a Windows download that is malware, with no part of Janas in it (a LuaJIT interpreter and an obfuscated script), reported to GitHub on 6 November 2026. Janas is published only at github.com/prabanta-dev/janas, its releases and huggingface.co/prabanta-dev; the packages' READMEs say so too.

## [2026-11-04] - Qwen3-Next whole on a card of its own, what the card does

- **Qwen3-Next whole on a card with memory of its own**: its files written before 2026 hold q, k, v and z in one matrix (`ssm_in`) and the betas and alphas in another, which Qwen3.5's shaders do not read; at load the card gets a copy of the first with its rows put in their order (the quantized rows moved, nothing recomputed) and the betas and alphas as float32 rows by head, the CPU keeping the file's. Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B runs every pass whole on the card: with the integrated Arc acting as a card of its own (`JANAS_GPU_COPY=1`) and an experts' tier of 1 GiB, janas-bench read a prompt of 256 tokens at 45-49 tokens/s in every row and wrote 4.8-6.7 tokens/s, 8.3-11.8 with MTP drafts greedy. The text is the CPU's for its first 50 tokens or so, then takes another way; its quality against llama.cpp is not measured yet.
- **janas-bench says what the card does**: under the table, the card (its memory of its own, the PCIe link it runs on and the fastest it could), whether every pass runs whole on it or why not, and the experts' tier: its slots and memory, the share of the experts asked for found there, read into it and computed by the CPU. `--gpu-profile` adds the card's own timestamps of each phase of a pass (as `JANAS_GPU_TOKEN_PROFILE=1`). The note on a card of its own now says that the threads also compute a mixture's experts the card does not hold.
- **The cache in memory gives way to the card's tier**: an expert just read into the card's tier is the first the memory cache replaces, its place going to the experts the CPU computes instead of holding the same expert twice; `JANAS_GPU_DEMOTE=0` keeps the old order. Not measured yet.

## [2026-11-04] - The prompt's tiles on 512 bits, a GPU by name, building on Windows

- **The prompt's tiles on 512 bits** where AVX-512 VNNI is (Zen 4 and Zen 5, Intel from Ice Lake, Sapphire Rapids and the Xeons after it): the products of a prompt, a block of tokens against the weights, are bound by the arithmetic and not by the memory, and on these processors a register of 512 bits holds four weights of sixteen rows instead of eight, against a token's four activations broadcast from memory into each `vpdpbusd`. Every tile type has them: Q4_K, Q5_K, Q6_K and Q6_K_P's planes, Q3_K, Q2_K, IQ4_XS, the IQ types with grids, and the types in blocks of 32 (Q8_0, Q4_0, Q4_1, Q5_0, Q5_1, IQ4_NL, MXFP4, Q2_0). Each lane is still a row doing the same exact integer sums and the same float operations in the same order, so the results are the 256-bit tiles' to the bit: so under Intel's SDE as an Ice Lake and as a Sapphire Rapids, with a mutation of every new function caught by the tests. Their speed has not been measured on such a processor yet; `JANAS_KERNELS=256` keeps the 256-bit tiles, to compare. The reports' kernels then read `avx512-vnni (512-bit tiles)` or `avx-vnni (512-bit tiles)`.
- **A GPU by its index or its name**: `JANAS_GPU_DEVICE` takes, besides `integrated` and `discrete`, a number (the device of that index in Vulkan's list, from 0, as `vulkaninfo --summary` counts them) or part of a name, any case (`JANAS_GPU_DEVICE=V100`), for a machine with two cards (issue #27: a Tesla V100 beside an RTX 4070, the 4070 taken first). When no device answers, the message says which was asked for.
- **janas-bench on a card with memory of its own** says that every pass of the model runs whole on the card in every row, the threads only feeding it, and how to measure the CPU alone (`JANAS_GPU=0`) or the products shared with the CPU (`JANAS_GPU_WHOLE=0`): a "12 threads" row read a prompt at 834 tokens/s where the CPU alone read 152 (issue #54). The model's description says so too. The cache column shows `-` where no expert was asked for, a dense model's, instead of 0.0%.
- **janas-bench's `--ctx`** too small for `--prompt` and `--gen` now says how many tokens it needs: the prompt, the generated tokens twice, 64 more (issue #27). The model's line is no longer cut short (issue #47), nor janas-chat's banner.
- **Power on a desktop**: janas-try took a wireless mouse's or keyboard's battery for the machine's and said a desktop ran on battery (issue #53); the engine counts a USB-C source online as mains, for laptops fed through USB-C whose adapter entry stays offline. janas-try lists a GPU once when two drivers' files list it twice (issue #54).
- **Building on Windows itself**: `build.bat` runs `build.sh` in MSYS2's MINGW64 shell (`build.bat setup` installs gcc, git, glslang and the Vulkan headers from MSYS2), and `build.sh` recognizes that shell and UCRT64's, with their gcc. It is new and has not been run on Windows yet. `environ` is declared only where MinGW's headers do not make it a macro (issue #40).

## [2026-11-02] - Ollama's API, AVX-512 VNNI kernels, builds with gcc 15

- **Ollama's API in janas-server** (issue #52): `/api/chat`, `/api/generate`, `/api/tags`, `/api/show`, `/api/ps`, `/api/version`, `/api/embed` and `/api/embeddings` beside OpenAI's routes, so that the programs that know a local model only as Ollama's find Janas-LLM with `--port 11434`; VS Code's chat finds the models with the first routes and talks to `/v1/chat/completions`. The requests take the same path to the engine as OpenAI's: pictures, tool calls with their arguments as objects, `think`, `format`, the options; the replies are streamed as lines of JSON by default. `/api/show` says what the model can do (tools, vision, thinking, filling in), from two new functions of the library, `janas_llm_uses_tools` and `janas_llm_sees`. Tried with Ollama's own Python client and Qwen3-4B: chat whole and streamed, a function called and its result used, reasoning apart, `generate`, an answer held to a schema; a picture, two calls in one reply and embeddings with `curl`.
- **Special tokens in raw prompts**: a raw prompt (`/v1/completions`, `/api/generate` with `raw`, `janas_llm_chat_prompt`) reads the model's special tokens written in it as those tokens, as llama.cpp and Ollama do. Editors' extensions write their own prompt for filling in code, `<|fim_prefix|>` and the rest included; read as text, the model never saw where to stop. In VSCodium with Continue 2.0.0 and Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B, through Ollama's API: a docstring written and accepted, a function's body completed at the cursor (with Continue's `modelTimeout` raised from its 150 ms); Continue's command-line agent read a small Gambas3 project and wrote a new one in its style.
- **Reports say the processor's instructions and the kernels chosen**: `janas-bench`, `gpu_check`, `janas-chat`'s `/about` and `janas-server`'s first lines give the instructions of the processor that matter to a model - AVX2, AVX-VNNI and its INT8 kind, the AVX-512 kinds, AVX10 and its version, AMX; dotprod, i8mm, SVE and SME on ARM - each where the system enables it, and the kernels Janas chose (`avx2`, `avx-vnni`, `avx512-vnni`, or AVX2 with VNNI left out by `JANAS_KERNELS`); the model's description says the kernels too. Under Intel's SDE they read right for Ice Lake, Sapphire and Granite Rapids, Arrow and Lunar Lake, Diamond Rapids.
- **Anthropic's Messages API in janas-server**: `/v1/messages` and `/v1/messages/count_tokens`, whole and streamed as Anthropic streams them, with tools, pictures, thinking and stop sequences, for Claude Code and the clients made for it; the API key also as `x-api-key`. Claude Code 2.1.311 ran against Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B and wrote a Gambas3 project from another. A request that only counts tokens no longer fails after a reply a client left unread (`janas_llm_chat_begin` leaves it where it is).
- **`janas-files`, your files as a service** (issue #52): what a folder holds, what a file says, the files found by name or by the text they hold; and, asked of you every time, a file written or edited, a folder made, a copy of what it replaces kept first under `~/.local/share/janas/files-backup/`. It reaches the home and the folders listed in `~/.config/janas/files-dirs.txt`, every path followed to its end before it is judged, and never the private files - keys, passwords, tokens, the browsers' data, the shells' histories, `.env` - nor deletes anything. In `janas-chat` with Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B it read a Gambas3 project and explained it, then wrote another in its style, each write confirmed. Linux only for now.
- **`janas-chat` lets a call that failed run again once something changed**: a call that failed is not run again in the same exchange, but a write that succeeds in between (the folder made that the file needed) clears that memory.
- **Ollama's streamed lines say `done: false`**, as Ollama's do: Cline's client refused every line without it and waited. With it, Cline 4.1.26 in VSCodium read the same Gambas3 project and wrote the new one through `/api/chat`.
- **AVX-512 VNNI kernels**: the processors with AVX-512 VNNI but not AVX-VNNI (Zen 4, Intel from Ice Lake and Tiger Lake to before Alder Lake and Sapphire Rapids) took the plain AVX2 kernels; every weight type's products, Q6_K_P's planes and attention's scores are now compiled a third time with `vpdpbusd` and `vpdpwssd` in their EVEX form on 256 bits, chosen at start where AVX-VNNI is missing (`JANAS_KERNELS=avx2` leaves them out). Under Intel's SDE as an Ice Lake they give the reference's bits; their speed has not been measured on such a processor yet.
- **Builds with gcc 15** (issues #50 and #51): the copy of a web search hit's title and address is bounded, and gcc's format-truncation warnings are shown without stopping the build, since each version of gcc guesses them differently.

## [2026-10-30] - AAC's low-delay kinds and USAC (xHE-AAC)

- **AAC-LD, AAC-ELD and ER AAC LC**, the kinds of calls and conferences: the error resilient syntax (the elements the channel configuration says, TNS's data after the gain control's flag), AAC-LD's frames of 512 and 480 samples and its low-overlap window, AAC-ELD's low-delay filter bank (an inverse transform over four frames, its window from the standard's tables) in frames of 512 and 480. Against FFmpeg on the ISO conformance streams FATE uses (er_ad6000np, 5.1 AAC-LD; er_eld1001np, 2000np and 2100np, AAC-ELD, the last in frames of 480): the same samples within 2.1e-7 (136 dB), all 471 files of the decoders' tests otherwise the same to the bit. Refused with a reason, as FFmpeg refuses them: ELD's low-delay SBR, the error resilience tools (HCR, RVLC, VCB11), error protection.
- **USAC (xHE-AAC) in the frequency domain**: its configuration and elements, the spectrum's context-adaptive arithmetic code, scalefactors, noise filling, mid/side and complex stereo prediction, TNS, frames of 1024 and 768 samples. On the ISO/IEC 23003-3 conformance streams with their ISO references (Fd_1_c1_0x03 and 0x04, Fd_2_c1_0x03 and 0x05, Fd_2_c1_Tns_0x04): within one step of their 16 bits; Fd_2_c1_Ms_0x01 and 0x04 against FFmpeg within 1.2e-7; a loudness-normalized stream within 61-76 dB of its references once their gain (+8, 0 and -7 dB) is applied. On a stream encoded by exhale, with noise filling, short windows and stereo prediction, FFmpeg's output (its reference in FATE) within one 16-bit step once the first channel's noise starts from FFmpeg's seed, 0: Janas starts it from the standard's, 0x3039. Not tried on a stream: the prediction's imaginary part, frames of 768, LFE. Refused with a reason: the linear-prediction coding of speech (LPD), SBR within USAC, the time-warped MDCT.

## [2026-10-30] - Recordings of any length, Whisper large-v3, speech in the chat and in Node

- **No limit on a recording's length**: the decoders hand their samples on as they make them, a stream decodes on a thread of its own and resamples to the bit as the whole resampler does, and a recording too long for memory is read in passes over the stream (peak, voice activity, the mel's maximum, the windows, the speakers' filter banks) with the same result as read whole (`JANAS_SPEECH_STREAM=1` forces it, `0` the memory). An MP3 of six hours (48 kHz stereo, 46 s of Italian every ten minutes), whisper-small with chunking and diarization: the version before refused it; read as a stream, 908 MB at most against 3,288 MB read whole, the same text, segments and turns to the byte, all 36 repetitions written with their times and one speaker found. The 471 files of the decoders' tests decoded to the same bits as before, every one of the 230 read as a stream the same as decoded and resampled whole.
- **Who speaks when in long recordings**: past 2000 windows (about 25 minutes of speech) the speakers are grouped by chunks, as NeMo's long-form clustering does: each chunk reduced by k-means to 64 centroids, the centroids grouped (again by chunks while they are too many), then each window given the group whose centre is nearest, so that memory and time grow with the length and not with its square. Shorter recordings are grouped as before, to the bit. On made embeddings of four speakers (`tests/test_diarize`), 2000 windows: by chunks 0 to 2 windows wrong where the whole grouping has 3 to 9 at the highest noise tried; without the last step, 173 to 209.
- **Whisper large-v3 and large-v3-turbo**: `hf2jns_whisper` computes the 128 mel filters their preprocessor's configuration leaves out, as librosa does, equal to whisper.cpp's to the bit. Against whisper.cpp the most likely token agrees on 29 of 29 positions (large-v3), 30 of 30 (turbo) and 28 of 28 (small.en).
- **Chunking keeps nearby speech together**: the stretches of speech found by Silero VAD are read in windows of up to 30 seconds, the pauses between them included, as WhisperX does; each stretch was a window of its own, and short ones came out as fragments (John F. Kennedy's 11 seconds as "ASK! | Knock!"), now as the recording read whole.
- **`/audio` in janas-chat**: `/audio [translate] <file>` transcribes (or translates into English) a recording with the Whisper model beside the language model - turbo first, then small, large-v3, any other - or `--speech-model`; its segments are shown as they come and its text goes with the next message, headed by the file's name, length and language.
- **Speech and pictures in the Node module**: `janas.openSpeech(path)` and `speech.transcribe(bytes, options)` (words, chunking, diarization with known speakers, an `AbortSignal`) on an engine thread of their own, and `chat.image(bytes)`; types in `janas.d.ts`.

## [2026-10-30] - The GPU's products the CPU's to the bit on older AMD GPUs

- **`fma()` rounded once where the driver splits it** (issue #48): Mesa's RADV lowers every 32-bit `fma` into a multiply and an add, two roundings, on the AMD GPUs before RDNA2 - Polaris, Vega, the Renoir and Cezanne APUs, RDNA1 - even where the shader asks for one operation, so the GPU's products came out a unit in the last place off the CPU's on about half the rows, and the self-check gave them to the CPU. When the GPU opens, a probe now computes `fma()` on 1,024 triples where one rounding and two differ; where the driver splits it, every shader that calls `fma()` is built from a variant whose `fma()` is rounded once through float64 (the product exact in a double, the sum rounded to odd, then to a float: Boldo and Melquiond's method; the same as the C library's `fmaf` on 200 million triples), provided the variant passes the same probe. On the Intel Arc of the development laptop, with the variants forced (`JANAS_GPU_XFMA=1`): `gpu_check`'s 3,980 products and 96 BF16 ones the CPU's to the bit.
- **Float conversions held exact in the shaders**: Mesa's NIR took `double(float(i))` for `double(i)`, the integer's rounding to a float lost; every conversion is now decorated as `fma()` already was (`tools/spv_fma.c`).

## [2026-10-29] - Speech in every sound file OpenAI accepts

- **Word times** (`timestamp_granularities[]=word` in janas-server's transcriptions, `words` in the library's parameters with `janas_llm_speech_words` and `janas_llm_speech_word`, the FreeBASIC header too), found as OpenAI's open-source Whisper finds them: the window's text decoded again keeping the attention of the model's alignment heads over the sound (from the checkpoint; OpenAI's default, the decoder's second half, without them), its softmax standardized and median-filtered, the path of least cost through it by dynamic time warping, tokens joined into words as OpenAI's tokenizer joins them (characters for Chinese, Japanese, Thai, Lao, Burmese and Cantonese), punctuation joined to its word, OpenAI's corrections at pauses and segment ends, and the next window starting at the last word's end. On John F. Kennedy's 11 seconds the words end where whisper.cpp's token times (the same method, in half precision) put them, within 20-40 ms.
- **Who speaks when** (`response_format=diarized_json` with `known_speaker_names[]` and `known_speaker_references[]`, as OpenAI's `gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize`; `diarize` and `janas_llm_speech_turns` in the library): WeSpeaker's ResNet34 speaker embeddings, converted from its ONNX model by a new tool, `wespeaker2jns`, run over Kaldi's filter banks computed as Kaldi computes them (1.6e-4 from kaldi-native-fbank; the embedding at a cosine of 0.9999993 to onnxruntime's), on windows of 1.5 s of the speech; WeSpeaker's spectral clustering (a k-nearest affinity, the Laplacian's eigengap for the number of speakers, k-means; the eigenvectors by Janas's own Householder and QL); each word the speaker of the windows over it, turns of consecutive words, known speakers named by their recordings. On 50 seconds spoken by three synthetic voices: ten turns of ten, the three speakers found, the known ones named; one speaker in a recording of one.
- **Chunking by voice activity** (`chunking_strategy` `auto` or `server_vad` with its threshold, prefix padding and silence, as OpenAI's; `chunking` in the library): Silero VAD v5, converted from its ONNX model by a new tool, `silero2jns` (a protobuf reader of its own, the 16 kHz model's weights in single precision), run as its model computes it (the STFT's basis, four convolutions, an LSTM carried from window to window) and its stretches of speech found as Silero's `get_speech_timestamps` finds them; only those are read, Whisper's windows within each, the text before carried on. Its probabilities agree with onnxruntime's on the same model to 1.7e-6; 46 seconds of Italian transcribed in eleven segments that follow the pauses.
- **Transcriptions streamed, with log-probabilities**: `stream: true` gives server-sent events as OpenAI's transcription models do (a `transcript.text.delta` for each segment as soon as it is final, `transcript.text.done` with the text and the usage), `include[]=logprobs` each text token's log-probability and bytes; in the library a callback for each final segment, a flag that stops a transcription between windows, and each token's log-probability. With the `openai` SDK: an M4A of 46 seconds streamed in ten deltas, words with their times, the logprobs of John F. Kennedy's 25 tokens.
- **A text that repeats itself is found as OpenAI's Whisper finds it**: its compression ratio, its bytes over their zlib-compressed bytes, above 2.4 has the window decoded again at a higher temperature (whisper.cpp's entropy of the tokens was used before); the compressed size from Janas's own deflate (`src/common/deflate.c`), identical to zlib's at its default level on 3,300 texts against Python's zlib (random bytes, words, loops, Italian, up to 32 KiB and over several blocks); `compression_ratio` in verbose_json's segments and in `janas_llm_speech_segment_info`.
- **Faults found by fuzzing the decoders under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer, fixed**: an Opus packet whose frames were empty concealed a frame of no samples and wrote before the CELT decoder's buffer (now no more is concealed than the packet's TOC says, as libopus does); an MP3 frame with more main data than the bit reservoir holds; ALAC's predictor and SILK's shifts of negative values in wrapping arithmetic made explicit; a FLAC header's skip.

- **Sound files read by Janas's own decoders**, for speech to text: FLAC (every kind RFC 9639 describes), MP3 and the other MPEG audio (MPEG-1, 2 and 2.5, Layers I, II and III: the bit reservoir, joint and intensity stereo, mixed blocks, free format, LAME's gapless trim), Ogg with Vorbis (floors 0 and 1, every residue), FLAC or Opus, and Opus itself - SILK in its exact fixed point with libopus's resampler, CELT, hybrid frames, the changes of mode between them, lost frames concealed as libopus conceals them, multistream with its channel mappings. Against FFmpeg's decoders and the reference streams: FLAC identical on 21 files; MPEG at 114 to 127 dB on 37 files and on ISO 11172-4's conformance streams, within 16-bit rounding of their reference PCM; Vorbis at 130 dB (91-99 dB on floor 0, where FFmpeg computes the line spectrum in single precision); Opus with the range coder's state identical, frame after frame, on every official test vector, SILK identical to the sample and CELT at 86 dB against Debian's libopus (139 dB once its approximate exp2 is taken into account). A Vorbis stream of a single page is now trimmed at its end only, as libvorbisfile does (FFmpeg drops its last 256 samples).
- **MP4, M4A, MOV, AAC and WebM, read by Janas's own decoders too**, so every format OpenAI's transcriptions accept is read: AAC LC, Main (its backward-adaptive prediction rounded as the reference does) and LTP, HE-AAC's spectral band replication (also downsampled, at the core's rate) and HE-AAC v2's parametric stereo, coupling channels, pulses, noise substitution, intensity and mid/side stereo, temporal noise shaping, frames of 1024 and 960; AAC in MP4 (sample tables and movie fragments, the edit list trimming the encoder's delay and padding) and in ADTS frames; ALAC; MP3, Opus, FLAC and PCM in MP4 and MOV; Matroska and WebM with sizes unknown (as browsers record them) and every lacing, with Opus (its codec delay and discard padding), Vorbis, FLAC, AAC, MPEG audio, ALAC and PCM. Against the MPEG-4 conformance streams FFmpeg's tests use (LC, Main, pulses, coupling channels, 96 kHz, 5.1, HE-AAC v1 and v2, downsampled SBR): 127 to 138 dB against FFmpeg, which itself is within 16-bit rounding of the reference PCM; ALAC, FLAC and PCM identical; AAC from FFmpeg's encoder in M4A, ADTS and Matroska 135 dB. Where FFmpeg's command line leaves the encoder's padding at the end (the edit list's length, Matroska's codec delay), Janas gives the source's exact length. AAC's low-delay (ER AAC LD, ELD) and USAC kinds are refused with a reason. Fifteen small clips, one of each format, in `tests/test_audio.c` with FFmpeg's decoding of them, every truncation and thousands of corrupted copies read or refused, clean under the sanitizers.

Against whisper.cpp on the same weights: the logits at a cosine of 1.000000, the same spectrograms, the same segments and times on 46 seconds of Italian but for two words, a comma and one time 40 ms apart; the tokenizer (GPT-2's expression, new in `tokenizer.c`) splits as Hugging Face's. - **Pictures in, with Gemma 4 12B**: its vision has no blocks - each patch of 48x48 pixels layer-normed, through one dense layer, normed, plus the embeddings of its column and row, normed, then projected - converted from its 100 MB of the checkpoint and run in `vision_gemma_u.c`, the same as llama.cpp's to a cosine of 1.000000; its pictures attended both ways as the 26B's. It told the shapes, read "JANAS 42" and described an abstract picture.
- **Pictures in, with Gemma 4 26B-A4B**: its larger encoder (27 blocks of 1152, standardized output) converted the same way, at a cosine of 0.999998 against llama.cpp's. In its language model a picture's tokens attend to one another both ways, as it was trained: the attention takes, token by token, the last position it sees (`janas_attn_set_last`, checked against a double-precision reference with a picture across calls and chunks), a prompt is never cut inside a picture, and a model whose encoder says so reads prompts in blocks that hold a picture whole; those passes stay on the CPU's attention. It describes a picture as llama.cpp's does.
- **Pictures in, with Gemma 4 E2B and E4B**: their vision encoder converted from the original checkpoint by `hf2jns_vision` (of Gemma's checkpoint only the vision tower is read: 338 MB for E2B), run as the model's own code does - the picture sized to at most 280 tokens as its processor does, RMS norms, every linear clamped to its bounds, a 2D rotary embedding of the patches' columns and rows, 3 x 3 pooling - and the pictures' tokens given the padding's per-layer inputs as the reference does. Against llama.cpp's encoder on the same pictures: a cosine of 0.999999 on average. Both models told a red circle and a blue square, a green triangle, and read "JANAS 42" off a picture of text.

The vision encoder is converted from the original checkpoint by a new tool, `hf2jns_vision`, into a `.vision.jns` beside the model, and runs as the model's own processor and code do: pictures resized as Pillow does (to the byte), Janas's own PNG and JPEG decoders (the same pixels as Pillow on 295 pictures of every kind), the ViT's output against llama.cpp's at a cosine of 0.999997; the pictures' tokens get their row and column as positions (the interleaved multimodal RoPE). With Qwen3.5-0.8B: "a red circle on the left and a blue square on the right", in the library, janas-chat and janas-server alike, and the same answers as llama.cpp's.
- **Code interpreter** (`--code-interpreter`, off by default): the model writes Python and the server runs it in a cage of its own (`src/common/sandbox.c`: user, mount, PID, network, IPC and UTS namespaces without privileges, the system's `/usr` read-only, only `/mnt/data` writable, no network, threads but no new processes, a seccomp filter, limits on memory, file sizes and open files), one Python session per container that keeps its variables like a notebook, figures saved as PNG; `code_interpreter` in the Responses API (with `code_interpreter_call` items and `container_file_citation` annotations) and in the Assistants (steps with logs and images as Files, `file_path` annotations), and the Containers API (nine operations). With the `openai` SDK and Qwen3-4B: computations, a CSV summed, a chart downloaded, variables kept; links planted by the code to the user's files never followed; the cage tried against code that wants out in `tests/test_sandbox.c`.
- **Assistants** (`/v1/assistants`, `/v1/threads`, messages, runs, run steps; 23 operations): assistants with functions and `file_search` over this server's vector stores, threads and their messages (files attached for `file_search` go into the thread's own store), runs in the background through the same code as the Responses API - function calls wait for `submit_tool_outputs`, searches become steps, answers become messages with `file_citation` annotations; `cancel` stops the model at its next token, and a run cut by a stop goes on after the restart. Runs stream their events with `stream: true`, the text as the model writes it. With the `openai` SDK and Qwen3-4B, all of these, polled and streamed. Not yet: `code_interpreter`, images.
- **janas-server stops at once with a request running**: Ctrl+C waited for the whole reply of every request in progress, even when its client had gone (a request without a stream never looks at its connection), so a stop could take minutes. The clients' requests, running and waiting, now end at their next token (or block of the prompt read), new ones are refused, and the server waits at most 2 s for them to answer: a client still there gets "the server is stopping" (as an error, in a stream too); batches and evals go on where they resume at the next start. With Qwen3-4B and a reply of 2,000 tokens running: stopped in 0.1 to 0.2 s, client gone, waiting, or reading a stream.
- **JSON output held to a string's `pattern`**: classes, groups, alternatives and repetitions as a grammar, a quote, a backslash or a control character written escaped, the match written from the string's start (an unanchored pattern let the model write on without ever reaching it); patterns it cannot hold (anchors inside, back references, look-arounds) leave the string free as before. Qwen3-4B at temperature 1, 100 replies to a schema of six patterns (a plate, a code, a phone number, a hashtag, a quoted motto, a Windows path): 100 valid.
- **Evals** (`/v1/evals`, twelve operations): an eval's graders - string checks, text similarity (fuzzy match, BLEU, GLEU, METEOR, ROUGE, cosine) and the server's own model as a judge that labels or scores, held to its answer by a JSON schema - run on rows given in the request or in a JSONL file, the samples written by the model from a template or brought by the rows; runs go on in the background behind every client's request, saved row by row, so they go on after a restart, and can be cancelled. With the `openai` SDK and Qwen3-4B, all four kinds of grader, a cancel and a restart.
- **`file_search` in the Responses API**: the model searches the request's vector stores, answers from the passages and cites them as [n], which become `file_citation` annotations, with a `file_search_call` item holding the query and the results. With Qwen3-4B, the right file found and cited.
- **Vector stores** (`/v1/vector_stores`, sixteen operations, the file batches among them): text files cut into overlapping chunks, embedded in the background with `--embedding-model` and searched by their cosine, or by their words (BM25) without one; attributes and filters, scores from 0 to 1. With the `openai` SDK and Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B, each of three questions found its own file first.
- **HTTPS** (`--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`): the server listens itself, does the TLS handshake on the Mbed TLS it carries, and hands libmicrohttpd the connection in clear through a pair of sockets, so every request and stream is the same; the key is checked against the certificate at start. With `curl` and the `openai` SDK, four requests at once, clean under AddressSanitizer. Linux and macOS.
- **`web_search` in the Responses API**: the model searches Wikipedia in the language it chooses (English when that has nothing), answers from the pages' first sentences and cites them as [n], which become `url_citation` annotations, with a `web_search_call` item listing the query and the sources; `--no-web` refuses it. With Qwen3-4B in English and in Italian, the answers came from the pages and cited the right ones.
- **Batches** (`/v1/batches`, four operations): a JSONL file of requests checked, run one at a time behind every client's request (a chat reply in 0.12 s while a batch ran), each through the same code as the request it stands for, answered in an output file and an error file; saved after every line, so a batch goes on after a restart; cancelled or expired, what was done is kept. Tried with the `openai` SDK.
- **Uploads** (`/v1/uploads`, four operations): a file of up to 8 GiB in parts of up to 64 MiB, joined into a File on completion and checked against its size and MD5 (`src/common/md5.c`); an upload lasts an hour. With the `openai` SDK, 150 MiB in three parts came back the same.
- **Files** (`/v1/files`, five operations): uploads as the SDKs send them, with their purpose and expiry, kept in the store's directory under ids (a client's file name never a path), listed, read back after a restart, downloaded, deleted; 16 GiB in all at most, a gigabyte of disk left free; with `--no-store` in a directory removed at the end. The ground for batches, uploads and vector stores. Tried with the `openai` SDK.
- **JSON output held to a schema's numbers and formats**: integers to `minimum`, `maximum`, their exclusive forms and `multipleOf` (up to 50), exactly - a grammar of their digits that never begins a number it cannot end inside the bounds; other numbers to their bounds, without an exponent and with a fraction only where it cannot cross one; the formats `date`, `time`, `date-time`, `uuid`, `email` and `ipv4` as their grammar, through a small reader of regular expressions (`src/llm/schema_re.c`, there for `pattern` next); string lengths up to 4,096 instead of 64. Every integer from -1,200 to 1,200 checked against five schemas; Qwen3-4B at temperature 1, 100 replies to a schema with all of them: 100 valid.

- **One choice for how much quality the engine may give up for speed: `--quality high|medium|low`** (janas-chat, janas-server; `min_quality` in `janas_llm_params`, `quality` in the Node module, `Quality` in the FreeBASIC classes). With `low`, the default, the engine decides by the machine, as before: where the experts do not all fit in memory it reads them at four bits, and with less than a fifth of them in memory it leaves out the light ones not there rather than wait for the disk (Nemotron 3.5 Bolt with a 2.7 GB cache: 391 of 400 against llama.cpp where 396, 6% faster); with memory for every expert nothing is lowered. `medium` allows the four bits but leaves no expert out; `high` lowers nothing. In janas-chat, `--quality low` asked for also takes the catalog's levers measured faster on that model (the former `--profile fast`; `--profile` stays as the older name, `quality` meaning `high`). The single levers - `--bits`, `--head`, `--attention`, the experts per token - stay, and win over it.

- **janas-server no longer falls over on a text part that is not a string**: a message's `"text"` given as an array or an object (chat completions and responses) had its length taken for its bytes and was copied past its end, and the server crashed; it is now a 400 (`invalid_value`). A refusal part's text is read from its `refusal` field, as OpenAI writes it.
- **Web pages of other sites cannot use the server**: a page you visit could make the browser send requests to `127.0.0.1`, and with a name of its own resolved there (DNS rebinding) read the answers. The browser's `Origin` is now checked on every request: the server's own page passes, programs (which send none) are not concerned, any other page gets a 403 (`origin_not_allowed`) whatever the key; `--allow-origin ORIGIN` lets a web client of another origin in, with the CORS headers it needs.
- **MCP servers named in a request are reached at public addresses only**: every address the name resolves to is checked and the connection goes to one that passed, so loopback, private, link-local (the clouds' metadata), shared, multicast and reserved ranges are refused, IPv4 inside IPv6 judged as itself; `--mcp-allow HOST[:PORT]` names an MCP server of your own on this machine or the network.
- **Stored documents have limits**: at most 64 MiB each (a conversation that would grow past it gets a 400, `conversation_too_large`) and 1 GiB in all, the oldest going first; adding to a conversation joins the texts instead of reading the whole of it back and writing it again.
- **janas-chat no longer lets a model or a tool drive the terminal**: what a model writes and what MCP tools return were printed as they came, so an escape sequence in them could move the cursor, switch screens or set the clipboard (OSC 52); the control characters and the C1 controls are now taken out, and a reply's tabs are four spaces. A third party's tool call is shown whole when you are asked to allow it (it was cut at 400 bytes, and `[a]lways` said yes to what was not shown). `/load-config` freed the settings' text that options still pointed into (`/save-config` then read freed memory); resizing the window no longer counts as "no" to a question or closes a page; two MCP servers whose names make the same prefix (`a.b` and `a_b`) get different ones; the input history is readable by its owner only.
- **janas-get and the tools**: a download goes to `.part` and takes its name only once its SHA-256 is checked (an interrupted one could pass for the model), a file Hugging Face lists no SHA-256 for is said to be unchecked, and a redirect from HTTPS to HTTP is refused; on Windows the arguments of a `.cmd`/`.bat` (npx) are always quoted and those holding `"`, `%`, `!` or a newline refused, as cmd.exe would act on them; hf2gguf past 1,024 tensors, janas-nsm on a file with carriage returns only, and the version and the GPU's reason in build.sh's `-D` strings escaped.
- **CPU kernels**: a row ending inside a group with a shape its type cannot end in pads with zeros instead of copying past the row (the loaders refuse such shapes); the experts' bit planes are refused for any resident tensor (they could be sized but not unpacked); Q2_0's and MXFP4's tiles no longer read minima never written; NVFP4's scale table is filled at start rather than checked once a row.
- **GPU: fixes from the review**: the keep-alive's empty dispatch wrote a float near the end of the stage that carries a card's results back, from another thread and in no order with the copies (a card with memory of its own could see four bytes zeroed): it has a buffer of its own; on a GPU whose subgroups have fewer than 8 lanes the row shaders would step 0 blocks at a time, for ever: they are not made there, the products go the other ways and the whole pass is refused; the attention's workgroups take at most 16 query heads and, with sixteen-bit keys, 2,048 numbers of them; Qwen3.5's gated query under `JANAS_GPU_REPACK` cut every head from the first; `JANAS_GPU_MVID` below the block size left the experts' tables too short for the types that always go by rows; host memory is imported only with a coherent memory type; the rotations of `qk_rope`, `ix_prep` and `ix_pool` are rounded twice as on the CPU, never fused by a driver; the memory a card gives back is counted again; one command buffer freed. `gpu_check` on the laptop's Arc as before (3,980 products, 0 wrong), Qwen3.5-0.8B's whole pass 245 of 256 against llama.cpp.
- **Nemotron's context slides right, and its mixture with a small cache**: sliding the context (a long chat) turned back by the dropped positions keys that had never been turned, since Nemotron's attention has no RoPE: now they only move (`llm_slide`, 400 tokens, 100 dropped: the same most likely token, mean |diff| 0.21, the next 24 tokens agreeing for 22). With an expert cache under a fifth of the experts, the experts left out because they were not in memory had the others' weights brought back to a sum of one, where Nemotron's router gives five; the weights now go back to the router's own sum, and the threshold is a share of it. With 2.7 GB of cache, 400 tokens of C against llama.cpp: 391 of 400 at 6.9 tokens/s with the experts left out, 396 at 6.5 without.
- **A cache smaller than a block's experts**: with fewer than 128 slots a prompt's block asked for more experts at once than the cache holds, and failed; the requests are now cut to the slots there are (Nemotron, 100 MiB of cache, blocks of 64: the same bits as a token at a time, 64 of 64 against llama.cpp). An expert that found no slot because reads ahead held them all was handed over as a null pointer: the reads ahead are settled and the slot sought again, and failing that the request fails instead of crashing.
- **Smaller fixes in the engine**: a saved state with a recurrent log longer than its buffers or a base outside its text is refused; a pass that fails reading Gemma 4's per-layer inputs no longer loses the recurrent state first; a slide that cut the recurrent log went on replaying it from the wrong place, now the state goes on from the log's end; the MTP layer's own router and convolution copies are freed.
- **A model file must be what it says it is**: a JNS file's resident tensors are now refused unless their bytes are what their type and shape make (a crafted file, downloaded, could have the engine read past the region), the experts' gate, up and down matrices must all have the shapes the buffers are made for (only the gate's rows were asked), and a Nemotron layer's router may name only experts present in the file and at least as many as it chooses. A GGUF tensor with an empty dimension is refused, and `gguf2jns` refuses rows longer than 16 MiB (they overran its buffers), more layers than the engine reads, more experts used than there are, and metadata with arrays of arrays; `hf2jns_mtp` reads a safetensors header with every field checked. Every JNS file here opens as before, and one converted again comes out the same to the byte.
- **Smaller fixes in the engine's plumbing**: the expert cache sorted with a variable shared between caches, divided by zero on a file whose every slot is empty, and destroyed locks it had not made yet when an allocation failed; the NPU's graph cache now keys on the device too, is not used when the driver's version cannot be read, and two NPUs of one process write it without sharing a temporary name; a failed keep-awake no longer leaks when asked again; the thread pool returns NULL instead of ending the program when a thread cannot start.
- **Smaller fixes on the server and its clients**: a prompt of token ids with a token longer than 255 bytes read past a buffer; response ids made of zeros if the system's randomness failed; the MCP client's header lines capped at 64, so a call's own pushed the configured `Authorization` out; a redirect from HTTPS to HTTP on the same host kept the credentials (now dropped on any change of scheme, host or port).

## [2026-10-28] - NVIDIA's Nemotron 3.5 Bolt, and gpu_check asks what the engine asks

- **NVIDIA Nemotron-3.5-Bolt-74B-A9B on the CPU** (architecture `nemotron_h_moe`, NVIDIA's own 24GB GGUF: experts in IQ2_XXS trained at two bits, Mamba layers in NVFP4): 88 layers, each one part with its own norm and residual - 40 Mamba-2 mixers, 40 mixtures and 8 attentions without RoPE. Against llama.cpp, 400 tokens of C, the most likely token agrees at 395, mean |diff| 0.183; the tokens the same as llama.cpp's on seven texts. On the laptop's CPU, 12 threads, a token at a time with every expert in RAM: 6.8 tokens/s. What it took: the NVFP4 type (four sub-blocks of 16 with UE4M3 scales; dequantized as ggml to the bit, an AVX2 product the same bits as the reference); Mamba-2 (`model_mamba.c`: the conv with its bias, the scan in float, D, the gate and the grouped norm, on DeltaNet's two buffers, replay and log, so drafts work); the mixture in a latent space (the input brought down to 1,024 before the experts, their weighted sum brought up after), experts with relu^2 and no gate, routed by sigmoids with a bias for the choice, each layer with its own number of experts; Mistral's Tekken pre-tokenizer (`pixtral`). `gguf2jns` writes layers without experts, experts without a gate and a table as wide as the layer with the most, the others' slots absent; the reader takes them only as a tail with zero checksums.

- **The attention's way chosen once, so that a pass gives the same result every time**: the choice between the query as it is and at sixteen bits (26 October) was measured on the calls themselves, a band of positions at a time, and so changed from one pass to the next: the same tokens could come out a few hundredths apart, a block's not the same bits as a token at a time (Nemotron: 0 of 64 positions). Now it is measured once, before the first call, on made-up keys of the model's geometry at 1,024 positions and up (a token's calls to 65,536, a block's to 16,384), and kept in `~/.cache/janas/attn.txt` by processor, threads and geometry: the sixteen-bit way from the shortest context where it wins there and at every longer one. Blocks are again the same bits as a token at a time (Nemotron 64 of 64); Qwen3-4B after 4,096 tokens as before (writing 22.9 tok/s, prompt 98.0). `JANAS_ATTN=float` or `int16` fixes it.

- **Nemotron's NVFP4 on the GPU, and the NPU's graphs kept across sessions**: NVFP4 is b32_matvec.comp's eighth kind (a block of 32 is half an NVFP4 block, its two sub-blocks' dots in two fmas as on the CPU), so a prompt's blocks give the GPU Nemotron's Mamba projections too, the same bits as the CPU (blocks of 64, the fast prompt's tiles apart: those round as they do for every type, and agree with llama.cpp as the CPU does, 254 of 256); its whole pass on the GPU is not there yet. The NPU's compiled graphs are now kept in `~/.cache/janas/npu` by default instead of only where `JANAS_NPU_CACHE` said: their preparation is paid once, not at every session (Nemotron: 36 s the first time, 3.8 s after). The engine's choice of the NPU and janas-bench's rows already leave that preparation out. On Nemotron the NPU takes the Mamba, attention, latent and shared products of the prompt's blocks; on the laptop it reads them a little slower than the CPU (19.2 against 20.2 tok/s), so the engine leaves it off there.

- **`gpu_check` no longer runs on the GPU a product the engine would leave to the CPU** (issue #46): its "every row on the GPU" pass called the GPU without asking whether it takes the product, and one of its shapes starts a row into the buffer, two bytes off a 4-byte boundary for the blocks of 110, 210, 82, 50, 66, 74 and 98 bytes (Q3_K, IQ3_S, Q6_K, Q6_K_P, IQ1_S, IQ2_XXS/XS/S, IQ3_XXS). The engine never gives those to the GPU (`janas_gpu_can` wants their weights 4-byte aligned, as model files keep them); the Arc read them right anyway, AMD's driver did not, and gpu_check reported 164 wrong products on a Radeon 890M. Now they are counted as left to the CPU, as the engine computes them: on the laptop's Arc 3,980 products, 0 wrong, 220 left to the CPU.

## [2026-10-26] - The GPU's attention nearer the CPU's

- **The attention's scores the way this processor computes them faster**: in float over the query as it is, or over the query at sixteen bits (an integer sum), measured on the calls themselves - every 4,096 positions eight calls each way, a token's and a block's apart - in place of the sixteen-bit query only past 16,384 tokens of context. The two agree with llama.cpp alike: the most likely token at 2,961 of 3,200 positions with the sixteen-bit query, 2,963 in float (Qwen3 0.6B and 4B, Qwen3.5 0.8B and 2B, prose and code or instructions), mean |diff| 0.3033 and 0.3037. Qwen3-4B on the laptop's CPU, which takes the sixteen-bit way: a token written after 4,096 tokens 22.0 to 23.4 tok/s (the attention 10.7 to 8.2 ms), after 8,192 18.2 to 19.6; the prompts 95.9 to 99.6 and 74.0 to 78.7. `JANAS_ATTN=float` or `int16` fixes it.

- **A long prompt goes the way the machine reads it faster**: the GPU's whole pass, or the path without it (the CPU's attention, the products where they go), measured on the blocks themselves - every eight of the engine's blocks of positions one each way, in turn, and the faster for the rest - since the attention's cost grows with the position, and on the GPU it can grow faster or slower than on the CPU. On the development laptop's integrated Arc, Qwen3-4B: even from position 0 (8.51 and 8.52 ms a token), without it from 4,096 on (18.8 against 12.3 ms a token, at 12,288 41.6 against 25.0); a prompt of 16,384 tokens at 49.7 tok/s instead of 31.8. No threshold: a GPU with fast memory of its own measures its own way; `JANAS_GPU_LONG=0` keeps the whole pass, `JANAS_GPU_TOKEN=1` too.

- **The GPU's norms, the same bits as the CPU's, at a fraction of the time**: the sum of squares' two floats added in a tree (seven or eight steps) instead of each thread going through every thread's in turn - the norms and quantization (norm_quant.comp) 352 ms to 56 a pass of 192 tokens of Qwen3-4B, q's and k's norms with RoPE (qk_rope.comp) 225 to 52, a prompt of 2,048 tokens read with the GPU at 112 tok/s instead of 93; the same bits as before (Gemma 4 12B, 367 of 400 against llama.cpp). A tile of a block's tokens a workgroup in the attention, each key read once for them, was tried and left out: eight times slower, the GPU's attention bound by its arithmetic and shared memory, not by reading the cache.

- **The fast prompt's Q4_K and Q6_K tiles can sum as the CPU does, opt-in (`JANAS_GPU_TILES_EXACT=1`)**: a whole block staged per barrier, its integer sums, then the CPU's float accumulators - the same bits as the CPU on every product of Gemma 4 12B (the tiles as they are: errors of some 1e-6 of the largest output), and the 12B's blocks of 64 back to 235 of 256 against llama.cpp from 209. Not the default: this first version is several times slower than the tiles. `JANAS_GPU_COPY` is read by its value (=0 was taken as asked).

- **A dense feed-forward whose down matrix is in blocks of 32 takes the CPU's activation on the GPU too**: the deterministic SiLU or GELU quantized straight into the blocks of 32 (expert_act.comp's kind 1, as the experts in those types), in place of the GPU's e^x then a quantization apart (`JANAS_GPU_ACT_FLOAT=1` as before). Gemma 4 26B's dense branch (Q8_0): against llama.cpp 321 of 400 instead of 315, the CPU's own (mean |diff| 0.794 instead of 0.806); DeepNSM-1B Q8_0 within the noise (464 and 465 of 477).

- **Gemma 4 26B-A4B on the GPU's whole pass**: its mixture as the CPU computes it - the dense branch (the shared expert, weight 1, no gate) on the feed-forward's norm and normalized apart, the experts' input normalized by its own norm, the router's input the residual normalized without weights times its scales over sqrt(d_model) (norm_quant.comp multiplies the normalized value first), each expert's output scale on its weight (moe_topk.comp), the experts' sum normalized and the dense branch added before the sandwich norm - and its experts' types: Q5_1 down matrices 704 wide through b32_matvec.comp by expert (the CPU's bits), the last layer's Q8_0 ones through q8_0_matvec.comp, which takes the experts' table too now; GELU in the experts' activation. Against llama.cpp, 400 tokens, the experts through a tier of 1 GiB in the card's memory: the most likely token at 315 (the CPU 321), mean |diff| 0.806 (0.817), against the CPU 332 and 0.616; blocks of 4 the same bits as token by token; no error from the validation layer with synchronization. Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B as before (164 of 200, 2.904). The fast prompt's tiles round group by group: Gemma 4 12B, the most sensitive, in blocks of 64 at 209 of 256 against 234 exact (E4B 251 and 252, Qwen3-4B 233 and 237): to be made exact.

- **Gemma 4 E2B and E4B on the GPU's whole pass**: their per-layer embeddings - the inputs the CPU makes for each layer (the table is read from the file, never on the GPU) go with the block, and after each feed-forward the branch on the GPU, its f32 gate, GELU times the layer's inputs in a shader of its own with the CPU's operations, the f32 projection back, its norm, the output scale after it - and the layers that read another's keys and values (their queries alone); the feed-forwards' widths may differ layer by layer (E2B's 6,144 and 12,288). Against llama.cpp, 400 tokens: E2B Q4_K_M the most likely token at 386 (the CPU 391), mean |diff| 0.427 (0.470), against the CPU 388 and 0.428; E4B 392 (394), 0.281 (0.286), against the CPU 390 and 0.271; blocks of 4 the same bits as token by token, the same as a card with memory of its own, no error from the validation layer with synchronization in either.

- **Gemma 4's dense layers on the GPU's whole pass, and its norms the same bits as the CPU's**: the token's shaders take a layer's own geometry (Gemma 4's sliding-window layers, heads of 256, eight KV heads, a window of 1,024 kept in a ring, a RoPE table of their own; its full layers, sixteen heads of 512 on one KV head, split among four workgroups of four, V as K before its norm), V normalized without weights, scores not scaled, keys and values at sixteen bits as the CPU keeps them (the same cache for both, q in float, the scores float products), the sandwich norms of the attention's and the feed-forward's outputs and the layer's output scale, GELU in the activation's shader with the CPU's operations, the logits' soft cap after. Gemma's prompts move with any rounding of the products' inputs, so the norms now give the CPU's bits: the sum of squares in two floats, the mean rounded once, square root, reciprocal and amax / 127 correctly rounded, quantized as janas_q8k_quantize (the q and k norms, V's, the attention's output too; the experts a card's tier leaves to the CPU quantized the same way). Gemma 4 12B Q4_K_M, 400 tokens against llama.cpp: the most likely token at 367 (336 with the GPU's own rounding; the CPU 362), mean |diff| 1.01 (2.80; the CPU 0.90); blocks of 4 the same bits as token by token, of 64 too with the exact products (235 of 256, 0.97; the fast prompt's tiles 209 and 2.71: to be decided for Gemma); the same as a card with memory of its own; no error from Vulkan's validation layer with synchronization, both ways, tokens and blocks. The common models within the noise, before and after: Qwen3-0.6B 372 and 367 of 400 (mean 0.330 and 0.326; the CPU 375, 0.320), Qwen3.5-0.8B 383 and 381 (0.126, 0.127), Qwen3-4B 362 and 364 (0.733, 0.736), DeepNSM-1B 459 and 465 of 477 (0.086, 0.092), Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B with a tier of experts 165 and 164 of 200 (2.897, 2.904). Not yet: E2B and E4B (per-layer embeddings, layers sharing their keys and values), the 26B mixture.

- **The query at sixteen bits in the GPU's attention, opt-in (`JANAS_GPU_Q16=1`)**: the GPU's whole pass put q at eight bits for its integer products with the cached keys, a relative error some 4e-3 where the CPU keeps q in float; now, if asked, at sixteen, two eight-bit halves (q = 256 hi + lo), each score two integer products, 256 hi.k + lo.k. Heads up to 256 only (longer ones stay at eight bits: the sum could pass 2^31). Against the CPU, the most likely token the same at 463 of 477 positions instead of 457 and mean |diff| 0.072 instead of 0.090 (DeepNSM-1B Q8_0, as far from the CPU as the CPU is from llama.cpp), against llama.cpp 461 instead of 459; Qwen3-0.6B 234 of 256 instead of 228, 0.158 instead of 0.191; the same with a card with memory of its own (`JANAS_GPU_COPY=1`); no error from Vulkan's validation layer with synchronization checked. The default unchanged until its speed is measured.

## [2026-10-25] - Llama 3, and DeepNSM's explications

- **Llama 3 models, and a LoRA adapter merged into one**: `tools/hf2gguf` reads a Llama 3 checkpoint from Hugging Face (safetensors) and, if given, a PEFT LoRA adapter, merges the adapter (W + alpha/r B A in float, rounded to bf16, as PEFT's merge does on a bf16 model; the adapter's whole token tables, grown for its padding token, in place of the model's) and writes a GGUF of llama.cpp's "llama" architecture, which llama.cpp's own `llama-quantize` quantizes and `gguf2jns` converts: both engines then read the same weights. `gguf2jns` puts Llama's query and key rows back in the checkpoint's order (llama.cpp's converter permutes them for its rotation of adjacent pairs). The engine runs architecture `llama` (no norm on the query and key heads, Llama 3's RoPE factors), its pre-tokenizer (`llama-bpe`: numbers in runs of up to three digits) and its chat format (`<|start_header_id|>`, `<|eot_id|>`, `<|begin_of_text|>` at the start, also of raw text); on the CPU for now. Checked with DeepNSM-1B (Llama-3.2-1B with the adapter of arXiv 2505.11764, which writes a word's explication in the Natural Semantic Metalanguage): the merged weights the same to the bit as an independent merge in numpy, the file from Meta's weights the same byte for byte as from unsloth's copy; the tokens the same as llama.cpp's (476 of 476, digits, spaces, accents, Japanese and special tokens); the most likely token as llama.cpp's at 466 of 477 positions in Q8_0 (469 with 16-bit keys and values, the difference flat along the positions), 456 in Q4_K_M; prompt blocks the same bits as token by token. Its 149 benchmark words, Q8_0 on the laptop's CPU, DeepNSM's sampling: 57.8 tokens/s writing (llama.cpp on the same file and machine: 37.9 on 6 threads, 39.8 on 12), 270 reading the prompts (201, 144); the explications' legality by the paper's measure 5.36 +- 0.12 (llama.cpp 5.22, the paper 5.13), one sample a word, one run each. The GPU's whole pass takes them too since 26 October (it normalized every head of q and k; Llama has no such norms): against llama.cpp the most likely token agrees at 459 of 477 positions in Q8_0 (the CPU 466) and 450 in Q4_K_M (456), the same as a card with memory of its own, no error from Vulkan's validation layer with synchronization checked.
- **`janas-nsm`: explications in the Natural Semantic Metalanguage, counted, and an output head restricted to their words**: the command writes a word's explication with a model trained for it (DeepNSM: one word with its examples, or a batch of JSON lines, the model card's prompt or the paper's, its sampling) and counts it as the paper does - primes, function words and the other words, legality 10 (primes - molecules) / words, circularity (a form of the word in it) - or counts explications given (`--score`, no model). Two lists of primes: the paper's as its scores were computed (without "i" and "said": with them its records do not come back), and Goddard and Wierzbicka's 65 with their forms and phrases; NLTK's function words; the lemma by English suffix rules, a silent e put back, irregular forms. On the paper's 1,341 published records: circularity the same as spaCy's on all of them, primes and molecules on 1,337. `--strict` lets the model write only the primes, the function words, a list of molecules (its own default, or `--molecules FILE`) and punctuation: the library's `janas_llm_chat_vocab` (FreeBASIC `Chat.Vocab`) copies those tokens' rows of the output head into one matrix and the pass computes them alone, the others -inf (`janas_llm_model_set_vocab`; on the CPU, the copies being unknown to the GPU and the NPU; a pass whole on the GPU computes the whole head and is cut to the vocabulary after). The vocabulary's logits the same to the bit as the whole head's, token by token and in blocks, the whole head back unchanged after (`vocab_check`: DeepNSM-1B Q8_0 and Q4_K_M, Gemma 4 E2B with its soft cap; also under asan). DeepNSM-1B Q8_0 on the laptop's CPU, its 149 benchmark words, five seeds each way alternated: 3,841 tokens of 128,257, writing 57.9 to 72.1 tokens/s (the prompts as before, 266), legality 5.33 to 5.48 (+0.15, 95% interval +0.08 to +0.22, paired by word), primes +0.8 points, molecules -0.7, circularity and length within the noise. But the explications tell their word from its neighbours less well: another model, Gemma 4 E4B, ranking the word among four WordNet neighbours (sister senses, common words) from the explication, puts it first for 81.9% of the free explications and 75.8% of the strict ones (-6.0 points, -10.1 to -2.7; nine words lost, none won: leg, child, chat, chair, sour, candle, cut, elbow, sank; one seed, one judge), against 88.6% for WordNet's own glosses and 20.8% for another word's explication (chance 20%). The default writes freely. `test_nsm` counts explications by hand with both lists, UTF-8 punctuation, circularity and the lemma's rules. DeepNSM is CC BY-NC-SA: not shipped, converted by `tools/hf2gguf` for research.

## [2026-10-24] - A card with memory of its own runs the whole model

- **A mixture larger than the card's tier: the CPU's experts computed while the GPU computes its own, a round trip less a layer**: where the tier does not hold every expert a pass goes in a submission a layer, the CPU's plan between. The plan's last copies (experts read into the tier, the CPU's rows) went in a submission of their own, waited for: now in the next segment's submission, after a barrier. And a segment is two submissions: the GPU's products of the experts, not waited for, while the CPU computes the experts left to it; then their rows' copies and the rest of the layer, from the experts' sum, waited for (`cpu_part`; one submission as before where the CPU has none). Qwen3-30B-A3B UD-Q3_K_XL as a card of its own on the laptop's Arc, a tier of 1 GiB, 480 tokens one at a time: the CPU's plans from 43.8 to 12.5 ms a pass, the time between segments from 51 to 29, the second half from 9.0 to 9.9 tokens/s. The same logits to the bit; with drafts the same tokens as without, prompt blocks included, and no error of the validation layer, synchronization included. `JANAS_GPU_TOKEN_PROFILE=1` prints a pass's experts in the tier, read into it and computed by the CPU, and the CPU's and the cache's time.
- **Every pass whole on a discrete GPU**: on a card with memory of its own the model's passes - a prompt's blocks, a chain of drafts to verify, single tokens - go whole to the GPU, one submission each, where before every product's rows were shared with the CPU and the activations crossed the bus at each one (an Arc B580 read a prompt at 15.8 tokens/s, issue #43). The token's state - each attention layer's KV rows and scales, the Gated DeltaNet states and their log - is copied to the card's memory and has one owner at a time: the GPU after its pass, the CPU after its own, copied over only when the passes change hands (`janas_gpu_sync`), only the rows the GPU's layers use, so that an MTP block's and Gemma 4's assistant's own stay the CPU's. `JANAS_GPU_WHOLE=0` shares the products as before. Checked as a card of its own on the development laptop's Arc (`JANAS_GPU_COPY=1`), functional runs only: Qwen3.5-2B, Qwen3-4B and Qwen3.5-9B greedy, with and without drafts the same tokens, the text sensible; Qwen3.5-2B's 64 tokens 141 single tokens, 27 draft checks and 2 blocks whole on the GPU, the state sent three times and never read back; a prompt on the GPU and its tokens on the CPU (`JANAS_GPU_WHOLE=0`) right too. As on an integrated GPU, the whole pass's norms and attention sum in the GPU's order, so the text is not the CPU's to the bit. - **The whole pass's working buffers in the card's own memory**: on a card with memory of its own every intermediate of a pass is in its memory, where the shaders read and write it at its speed, and only what the host writes or reads - a block's inputs and position, the parameters once, the logits and hidden states - goes through a host-visible mirror (`janas_gt_sync_range` copies those ranges); before, every layer's activations crossed the bus. The same tokens to the bit as with the buffer in host memory (Qwen3.5-2B, Qwen3-4B, Qwen3.5-9B, as a card of its own; a prompt on the GPU and its tokens on the CPU too). Mixtures too: their routing, read and written by the host between layers, goes through the mirror as the rest; Qwen3-30B-A3B's prompt blocks whole on the GPU gave the same tokens through the mirror as without, also under the validation layer (`JANAS_GPU_TOK_MIRROR=1` puts the buffer in the GPU's memory behind a mirror on an integrated GPU too, to test it with the weights read in place; the verbose line says where the buffer is). `JANAS_GPU_TOK_HOSTBUF=1` keeps the host-visible buffer for any model. The staging buffers and a card's copies of the weights now declare the copies they take part in (transfer usage), which a driver may enforce. Not yet: mixtures larger than the card, Qwen3.8-Flash-Next on a card of its own.
- **A dense model's pass in one submission with its copies**: the copies of a pass's inputs (the embeddings, the rotations, the position words) to the card and of its logits and hidden states back to the mirror are recorded in the pass's own commands, with their barriers, instead of four submissions of their own around it, each waited for; the same tokens to the bit (Qwen3.5-2B with and without MTP drafts, Qwen3-4B against the host-visible buffer). Under the Khronos validation layer with synchronization checks, gpu_check on every type and Qwen3.5-2B's generation with drafts, on the shared memory and as a card of its own: no error.
- **A mixture larger than the card: a tier of experts in its memory, the CPU computing the others**: on a card with memory of its own a mixture's whole pass no longer needs every expert copied to the card.
